No. 994 of 1891-95 folio 13 b) states [[मध...मैथुनमेव च। भाग्यहीना (नः1) न लग्यन्ते मकाराः पञ्च दुर्लभाः".|'मद्यं...मैथुनमेव च । भाग्यहीना(नः ?) न लभ्यन्ते मकाराः पञ्च दुर्लभाः॥'.]]

[^1698]: एकः परमात्मा। ईश्वराः सप्त। असंख्या जीवाः । [[ब्रह्मविष्णुशिवसूर्यगणेशशक्ति. भैरवाश्वेश्वराः।|ब्रह्मविष्णुशिवसूर्यगणेशशक्ति-भैरवाश्चेश्वराः।]] पारानन्दे मते त्रयो मार्गाः। दक्षिणः । वामः। [[उत्तः।|उत्तरः ।]] तथैव [[गाथाइदाहरन्ति।|गाथामुदाहरन्ति ।]] दक्षिणादुत्तमं धाम [[घामादुत्तरमुत्तमम् ।|वामादुत्तरमुत्तमम् ।]] उत्तरादुत्तमं [[किंचिव|किंचिन्न]] [[ब्रह्माण्डमण्डले।|ब्रह्माण्डमण्डले ॥]] [[वामाचारी विषकारो मध्यमोसमभेदेन।|वामाचारो द्विपकारो मध्यमोत्तमभेदेन ।]] [[मयमैथुनमुदाभिर्युक्तोऽसाघुत्तमः|मद्यमैथुनमुद्राभिर्युक्तोऽसावुत्तमः]] स्मृत इति। [[मद्यमांसमस्य सुवामैथुनयुक्तो|मद्यमांसमत्स्य-मुद्रामैथुनयुक्तो]] मध्यमः। पारानन्दसूत्र (G.O.S.) [[Pp|pp]] 1-3, 13; compare कुलार्णवतन्त्र [[1.7-8|II. 7-8]] 'वैष्णवादुत्तमं शैवं [[शैवान|शैवाद]] [[वक्षिणमुत्तमम् ।|दक्षिणमुत्तमम् ।]] दक्षिणादुत्तमं [[वाम घामात|वामं वामात्]] सिद्धान्तमुत्तमम् । [[सिद्धान्तादुत्तम कौलं कोलात्परतरं नाहि..|सिद्धान्तादुत्तमं कौलं कौलात्परतरं नहि ॥'.]] वामाचार was possibly so called because woman [[(urat)|(वामा)]] played an important part in [[It|it]] or it was secretly practised (which is a crooked way). 

[[P1054]]that there are seven Lords [[(Isvara )|(Īśvara)]] viz. [[Brahma,|Brahmā,]] Viṣṇu, Śiva, Sūrya, [[Ganesa,|Gaṇeśa,]] Śakti and Bhairava, that individual souls are countless, that there are three mārgas [[(patha)|(paths)]] viz. Dakṣiṇa, [[Vama and Uttard,|Vāma and Uttara,]] each succeeding one being superior to each preceding one, that [[Dakṣiṇa-marga|Dakṣiṇa-mārga]] is the one declared in the Veda, [[smrtis|smṛtis]] and purāṇas, that the Vāma (way) is declared by the Veda and [[Agamas,|Āgamas,]] while the third (Uttara) is the one declared by the words of the Veda and of the Guru and that the Guruvākya is that of one's guru who is himself [[Jiran-mukta|Jīvan-mukta]] and who gives instruction as to [[&|a]] mantra. That sūtra further provides that the Vāmācāra is of two kinds, madhyama (middling) and [[utlama|uttama]] (best), that [[uttana|uttama]] is the one which is concerned with wine, sexual intercourse and hand poses, while madhyama is one where all five, wine, flesh, fish, [[mudra|mudrā]] and maithuna are resorted to. It should be noticed that the Tantras themselves designate the use of five makāras in worship as Vāmācāra and not their orthodox partisans of Yoga, as Heinrich Zimmer alleges in 'The art of Indian Asia' vol. I. p 130. Pārānandasūtra[^1699] prescribes that the disciple has to undergo [[dikṣā|dīkṣā]] (consecration) from a qualified guru, who instructs the disciple as to the mantra, who holds a mouthful of water in his own mouth and passes it into the mouth of the disciple that accepts the mantra while he [[gulpa|gulps]] down the water. This procedure applies if the guru is a brāhmaṇa, but if the guru is a [[ksatriya|kṣatriya]] he should recite the mantra in the right ear of the disciple. The [[Tantraraja-tantra|Tantrarāja-tantra]] provides that the guru should wait for 1, 2, 3, 4 or 5 years according as the intending disciple belongs to the four varṇas or to a mixed caste, should test [[bis|his]] qualities and devotion and then [[com municate|communicate]] to him the mantra; otherwise, both guru and disciple would come to grief ([[Tantrik|Tāntrik]] Texts, [[vol VIII, II, 37-38).|vol. VIII, II. 37-38).]] Most works on Tantra provide that the knowledge conveyed by the guru and worship with five makāras must be kept secret and if made public falling in hell is the result. Vide [[Parasurama kalpasūtra 1 12|Paraśurāma-kalpasūtra I. 12]] and [[Saktigangama-tantra.|Śaktisaṅgama-tantra.]][^1700] After undergoing [[dikṣā|dīkṣā]] and receiving the mantra the disciple has to follow the orders 

[^1699]: [[प्रत्यख उदबखं शिष्यं तविष्टमन्त्रमुपदिशेत् । मन्त्रं माहयित्वा गुबजलगद भूखा मन्त्र जप्त्वा शिण्यमुखे निक्षिपेत् । निगिरनेव शिष्यो मन्त्र गृहीयात् । एष माधणोप amaz referente aratot foto am p . 35 sõtras 12-16.|प्रत्यङ्मुख उदङ्मुखं शिष्यं तदिष्टमन्त्रमुपदिशेत् । मन्त्रं ग्राहयित्वा मुखजलगण्डूषं धृत्वा मन्त्रं जप्त्वा शिष्यमुखे निक्षिपेत् । निगिरन्नेव शिष्यो मन्त्रं गृह्णीयात् । एष ब्राह्मणोप-देशक्रमः। क्षत्रियश्चेन्मन्त्रं ग्राहयित्तोपदिशेद् दक्षिणे कर्णे। पारानन्दसूत्र p. 5 sūtras 12-16.]]

[^1700]: [[1700. are grant me a damgaregring i form an FT., red शुप्त्या, पाकच्याविरयः। पररामकल्पपत्र, 12 घेदशासपुराणानि स्वहा पेश्यामा: पंतशाम्भवी वियागुता कुलवधूरिन । समुतं कौलिकाचारमनुशान्ति देवताः। पासिनि प्रयच्छन्ति नाशयन्ति प्रकाशने शकिसान, वारा. 36.24-25.|आनन्दं ब्रह्मणो रूपं तच्च देहे व्यवस्थितम् । तस्याभिव्यञ्जकाः पञ्च मकाराः, तैश्चार्चनं गुप्त्या, पाकट्यादनिरयः। परशुरामकल्पसूत्र I. 12; वेदशास्त्रपुराणानि स्पष्टा वेश्याङ्गना इव । इयं तु शाम्भवी विद्या गुप्ता कुलवधूरिव ॥ सुगुप्तं कौलिकाचारमनुगृह्णन्ति देवताः। वाञ्छितं प्रयच्छन्ति नाशयन्ति प्रकाशने ॥ शक्तिसङ्गम, तारा० 36. 24-25.]] 

[[P1055]]of the guru till the former has [[&|a]] vision of the deity.[^1701] Guru is higher than all other men, mantra is higher than guru, the deity is higher than the mantra and the highest self is higher than the devatā. In order to attain siddhis the guru is to be served by disciples with devotion in all ways. There is only one way viz. bhakti (devotion) for those (disciples) that long for worldly pleasures, heaven or mokṣa, as the Śruti says 'there is no other way'. [[Jivan-mukti|Jīvan-mukti]][^1702] [[means to have a vision of the Deity worshipped' and one who is liberated, though living, is not tainted by his acts, whether meritorious or otherwise', This|means 'to have a vision of the Deity worshipped' and 'one who is liberated, though living, is not tainted by his acts, whether meritorious or otherwise'. This]] doctrine closely follows what is said in some of the Upaniṣads about the man who has realised brahma [[that he shakes|that 'he shakes]] off [[puṇya and papa|puṇya and pāpa]] and having cast aside the body he reaches the world of brahma; he does not return [[i.e.|i. e.]] he never again undergoes [[samsāra'.|saṁsāra'.]] One should strive for that stage. Therefore one who has obtained correct knowledge should become a bhakta (devotee). All these, [[viz.one in distress, the seekor|viz. one in distress, the seeker]] after knowledge, one who seeks some desired object and one who has obtained correct knowledge, are noble, but the man, who has knowledge about God, when he becomes a devotee, reaches the world of the Highest Self, as the Vedic words say 'one who knows brahma reaches the Highest'. With all this sublime philosophy as the background the Pārānandasūtra frankly provides that the guru, after completing the worship up to the offering of a handful of flowers and having offered into [[firo somo|fire some]] food, should make a collection of makāras, should again come to the place for worshipping the Deity and offer food into fire, should hand over to the neophyte a bowl for drinking wine, 

[^1701]: [[मन्त्रदीकावः माग्देवतासाक्षात्कारादुर्ववज्ञाप्यो भवति। सर्वेन्यः परो गुपये: परोमन्त्री मन्त्रात्परा देवता देवतायाः पर परमात्मेति भावयेत् । भोगस्वर्गापवर्गकांक्षिणां भक्तिरेवैक: Tout : aut eta gazt TREE Pp 6-7 sūtras 35, 38, 59. The saararitge III. 8 and VI. 15 have a pallacan ATO ry: You Magasyaray, This half verse also occurs ia 479. \#.31. 18.|मन्त्रदीक्षावतः प्राग्देवतासाक्षात्काराद्गुर्ववज्ञाप्यो भवति। सर्वेभ्यः परो गुरुर्गुरोः परो मन्त्रो मन्त्रात्परा देवता देवतायाः परः परमात्मेति भावयेत् । भोगस्वर्गापवर्गकांक्षिणां भक्तिरेवैकः पन्था नान्यः पन्था इति श्रुतिः। पारानन्द० pp. 6-7 sūtras 35, 38, 59. The श्वेताश्वतरोप० III. 8 and VI. 15 have 'तमेव विदित्वातिमृत्युमेति नान्यः पन्था विद्यतेऽयनाय'. This half verse also occurs in वाज. सं. 31. 18.]]

[^1702]: [[खोपास्यवर्शनं जीवन्मुक्तिः जीवन्मुक्तो न कर्ममिलिप्यते पुण्यैः पापै। नस gercrantai 7 \#49: Shri que elesa oferecerere straffa Haki strafaret स्वर्थाधिज्ञानिन उदारास्तत्रेशस्य ज्ञानी भक्त एव परमात्मलोक प्रामोति ब्रह्मविदामोति परमिति 

erai orang p. 9 sūtras 3–8. The words 7 garreta occur in , 4. VIII. 15 at the end and the words 'apparta Tre are 8. 39. ( TTT TA) at the very beginning. The words our... 7 are borrowed from after VII. 16-17 'uraren rgari: per para serait amit a Harl. Compare gogoare III, 1,3 (aar Per guggri fare facer: YA Higura), WT. VI. VIII, 13, Herrafatare IV. 22 ( MIA ESTE AVATART faca v And VII, 94) (... 4 a fogau).|स्वोपास्यदर्शनं जीवन्मुक्तिः। जीवन्मुक्तो न कर्मभिर्लिप्यते पुण्यैः पापैर्वा । न स पुनरावर्तते । न स भूयः संसारं सम्प्रपद्यते । तस्मात्तद्दर्शने यतितव्यम्। ज्ञानी भक्तो भवेत्। आर्त्तजिज्ञास्वर्थार्थिज्ञानिन उदारास्तत्रेशस्य ज्ञानी भक्त एव परमात्मलोकं प्राप्नोति ब्रह्मविदाप्नोति परमिति शब्दात् । पारानन्द p. 9 sūtras 3-8. The words 'न च पुनरावर्तते' occur in छा. उप. VIII. 15 at the end and the words 'ब्रह्मविदाप्नोति परम्' are तै. उप. (ब्रह्मानन्दवल्ली) at the very beginning. The words आर्त...ज्ञानी भक्त एव are borrowed from गीता VII. 16-17 'चतुर्विधा भजन्ते...उदाराः सर्व एवैते ज्ञानी त्वात्मैव मे मतम् ।'. Compare मुण्डकोप० III. 1. 3 (तदा विद्वान् पुण्यपापे विधूय निरञ्जनः परमं साम्यमुपैति); छा. उप. VIII. 13, महानिर्वाणतन्त्र IV. 22 (ब्रह्मज्ञाने समुत्पन्ने मेध्यामेध्यं न विद्यते । and VII. 94) (...न च लिप्येत).]] 

[[P1056]][[mudra,|mudrā,]] materials for dinner with [[oondiments|condiments]] and a courtezan and should instruct the [[neopbyte,|neophyte,]] that has accepted the three makāras (madya, mudrā and maithuna), as regards the kaula practices.[^1703] Then the Pārānanda-sūtra devotes two [[pagos|pages]] (16-17) to the kauladharmas taught to the neophyte, from which a few striking passages may be cited. “A young[^1704] [[courtezan ia Sakti|courtezan is Śakti]] incarnate, is brahma; women are gods [[ans|and]] the very life-breath and are ornaments (of the world); they should not be censured nor angered"; [[“after|'after]] worshipping the gods and gurus in the way laid down by the Veda and the Tantras [[&|a]] man does not incur sin if he drinks wine while remembering god or has sexual intercourse with a [[courtozan.|courtezan.]] He who partakes of wine and the rest merely for pleasing himself falls into a terrible hell. He who giving up the ordinances of śāstra acts as he pleases does not attain siddhi in this world, [[por|nor]] heaven nor the Highest goal [[( mokṣa).|(mokṣa).]] A worshipper should drink wine only up till his eyes do not begin to roll and up till [[bis|his]] mind does not become unsteady; to drink beyond that stage is bestial'. The [[Paranandasātra|Pārānandasūtra]] (pp. 70-71) describes the procedure of a festival (Utsavavidhi) among [[Tantrikas.|Tāntrikas.]] The mantra is [['Isvarātman, tava dasoham'|'Īśvarātman, tava dāsoham']], which may be given to even a cāṇḍāla or may be accepted from a cāṇḍāla. It is further provided that the followers of Vāma-mārga may employ the following mantras about the three makāras, that [[the best among )|(the best among )]]

[^1703]: [[गुरुः पुष्पाजल्यन्तामी विधाय हुला हविः प्रतिपश्यगारं मविश्य मकारत्रया मुररीकरय पुनदेवतागारमागत्य हुवा देवतां ससमस्तमण्डलामग्यपर्य नवशिष्यायालिपात्र समुदं सध्यानभोजनसम्भार सवेश्याकं प्रयच्छेत् ।...गृहीतमकारत्रयं नव्यं शिष्य कौलिक धर्मादपदिशेत् । पारानन्द pp. 15-16, sutras 56 and 63.|गुरुः पुष्पाञ्जल्यन्तामर्चों विधाय हुत्वा हविः प्रतिपन्नगारं प्रविश्य मकारत्रयमुररीकृत्य पुनर्देवतागारमागत्य हुत्वा देवतां ससमस्तमण्डलामभ्यर्च्य नवशिष्यायालिपात्रं समुद्गं सस्यानभोजनसम्भारं सवेश्याकं प्रयच्छेत् ।...गृहीतमकारत्रयं नव्यं शिष्यं कौलिक-धर्मानुपदिशेत् । पारानन्द pp. 15-16, sūtras 56 and 63.]]

[^1704]: [[स्वेच्छामतुमती शक्ति साक्षात् ब्रह्म न संशयः। तस्माता पूजयेडक्रया पचालङ्कारः भोजनैरिति रियो देवाः खियः प्राणाः श्रिय एव हि भूषणम् । श्रीणां निन्दा न कर्तव्या मचता: क्रोधयेदपि । इति । देवान् गुसन्ममम्यर्च्य वेदतन्त्रोक्तवर्मना। देवं स्मरन् पिबन् मचे वेश्या गच्छन दोषभाक् ॥ इति । सेवेदात्ममुखार्थ यो मद्यादिकमशानतः। स पाति नरकं घोरं मात्र कार्या विचारणा यः शास्त्रविधि ...पर गतिम् ॥ इति। यावत चलते हटियावत चलते मनः। तावत्पानं प्रकुर्वीत पशुपानमितःपरम् । इति। जीवन्मुक्तः पिवेदेवमन्यथा पतितो भवेत् । इति। पारानन्द. pp. 16-17 sutras 64, 65, 74-76, 80-81. Extravagant praise is bestowed on women in several tantras such as in Saktisaagama-tantra, Kalithanda 3. 142-144 and Tarakhanda 13.43-50 and in कोलावलीनिर्णयx.88. The hall verse 'त्रियो...भूषणम्! occurs in शक्तिसङ्ग्मतन्त्र, ताराखण्ड 23.10. The verse यः शात्र is भगवद्वीता 16. 23. For यावा...परम् compare कुलार्णवतन्त्र VII. 97-98, The qurfa states that every woman is born in the kula of the great Mother and so one should not beat a womad even with a dower even if sho be guilty of a hundred misdeeds, one should not siad the faults of women and should make known only their good points (XI. 64-65). Vido dlso कोलावलीनिर्णय X.66-69,|स्वेच्छानुमती शक्तिः साक्षाद् ब्रह्म न संशयः। तस्मात्तां पूजयेद्भक्त्या वस्त्रालङ्कार-भोजनैरिति ॥ स्त्रियो देवाः स्त्रियः प्राणाः स्त्रिय एव हि भूषणम् । स्त्रीणां निन्दा न कर्तव्या न च ताः क्रोधयेदपि ॥ इति। देवान् गुरून्नमस्कृत्य वेदतन्त्रोक्तवर्त्मना। देवं स्मरन् पिबन् मद्यं वेश्यां गच्छन्न दोषभाक् ॥ इति। सेवेतात्मसुखार्थं यो मद्यादिकमशान्तधीः। स याति नरकं घोरं नात्र कार्या विचारणा॥ यः शास्त्रविधि...परां गतिम् ॥ इति। यावन्न चलते दृष्टिर्यावन्न चलते मनः। तावत्पानं प्रकुर्वीत पशुपानमितःपरम् । इति । जीवन्मुक्तः पिबेदेवमन्यथा पतितो भवेत् ॥ इति । पारानन्द० pp. 16-17 sūtras 64, 65, 74-76, 80-81. Extravagant praise is bestowed on women in several tantras such as in Śaktisaṅgama-tantra, Kālīkhaṇḍa 3. 142-144 and Tārākhaṇḍa 13. 43-50 and in कौलावलीनिर्णय X. 88. The half verse 'स्त्रियो...भूषणम्' occurs in शक्तिसङ्ग्मतन्त्र, ताराखण्ड 23. 10. The verse यः शास्त्र० is भगवद्गीता 16. 23. For यावन्न...परम् compare कुलार्णवतन्त्र VII. 97-98. The कुलार्णव states that every woman is born in the kula of the great Mother and so one should not beat a woman even with a flower even if she be guilty of a hundred misdeeds, one should not find the faults of women and should make known only their good points (XI. 64-65). Vide also कौलावलीनिर्णय X. 66-69.]] 

sakla mantras for 'makūras' 

1057 

[[P1057]]Vāmācāras should resort to. They [[aro:|are:]] 'I take this holy nectar, which is a [[medioine ( antidote)|medicine (antidote)]] for [[samsāra,|saṁsāra,]] which is a [[meang|means]] of cutting off the [[sparos|snares]] by [[whioh|which]] the paśu (in man) is bound and which is [[deolared|declared]] by Bhairava' (this when taking the first [[i. o.|i. e.]] wine); 'I take this mudrā which is [['ucohista'|'ucchiṣṭa']] of the Lord (i. e. which has been first offered to God), which destroys the torments of the heart, which produces joy and which[^1705] is enriched with other food materials' (this when taking mudrā); [["I|'I]] take this divine young woman who has drunk wine, that always makes the heart full of bliss and that brings about my [[sādhana'|sādhanā']] (when taking one of the women that are brought [[together ).|together).]]

The Hindu Tantra works present two sides, one philosophical and spiritual, the other popular, practical and more or less [[magioal,|magical,]] which relies on mantras, mudrās, [[mandalas,|maṇḍalas,]] nyāsas, cakras and yantras as physical means to realize one's identity with the Supreme Power or Energy by concentration and as conferring extraordinary powers on the devotee. This may be illustrated by reference to two typical tantras, the [[Sāradātilaka|Śāradātilaka]] and the [[Mahanirvāṇa-tantra.|Mahānirvāṇa-tantra.]] The [[Mahānirvana-tantra,|Mahānirvāṇa-tantra,]] though it speaks of the five makāras as means of upāsanā and though it states that when the great Tantra is understood, the Vedas, [[Puranas|Purāṇas]] and śāstras are hardly[^1706] of any use, [[puta|puts]] forward the striking conception in IV. 34-47 that Parameśvara is one and to be described as sat, cit and [[ananda,|ānanda,]] that He is one without [[&|a]] second, is beyond the [[gunas|guṇas]] and is to be known from the [[Vedanta|Vedānta]] texts. It further on says that the best mantra is [['om sac gidekam brahma'(III. 14),|'om sac-cidekam brahma' (III. 14),]] that those who perform the upāsanā of the Highest Brahma do not require other means of worship [[(sādbanā);|(sādhanā);]] by [[stioking|sticking]] to this mantra man becomes brahma. In the 4th chap. however, the [[Mahāpariniryāṇa|Mahāparinirvāṇa]] starts by saying that Durgā is the highest [[praksti|prakṛti]] of Paramātmā, she has various names such as [[Kāli,|Kālī,]] [[Bhuvanośvari,|Bhuvaneśvarī,]] Bagalā, [[Bhairavi,|Bhairavī,]] [[Ohhin namastakā,|Chinnamastakā,]] that she is [[Sarasvati,|Sarasvatī,]] [[Lakṣmi|Lakṣmī]] and [[Sakti,|Śakti,]] that she [[aggumes|assumes]] various forms for securing the purpose of her devotees and for the destruction of [[demong.|demons.]] In the Kaliyuga perfection cannot be attained without following kula practices, which lead 

[^1705]: Mudrā has not here the sense [[of hand and finger posen'|of 'hand and finger poses']] but one of the meanings that will be noted under [[madra|mudrā]] [[later,|later.]]

[^1706]: [[किः किं पुराणैवाकिं शाहामिः शिव। विज्ञावेऽस्मिन्महातन्त्रे सर्वसिद्धीचरो WIN felfoafu|किं वेदैः किं पुराणैश्च किं शास्त्रैर्बहुभिः शिवे। विज्ञातेऽस्मिन्महातन्त्रे सर्वसिद्धीश्वरो भवेत् ॥ महानिर्वाण]] II. 31.

[[P1058]]to the knowledge of brahma and the man who possesses [[know ledge|knowledge]] of brahma is a [[liborated|liberated]] soul, though living (he is [[Jivanmukla).|Jīvanmukta).]] Then there is high praise (IV. 10 ff) of [[Dev!|Devī]] who is spoken of as the primordial [[Sakti (adyā paramā sakti )|Śakti (ādyā paramā śakti)]] and all gods including Śiva himself derive their powers from this Highest [[Sakti.|Śakti.]] A rather astounding statement is made in[^1707] the words 'as there was partaking of wine and the rest in Satya, Tretā and [[Dyāpara|Dvāpara]] yugas, one should do the same in Kali-yuga, but in accordance with the kula way, and that Kali does not affect those who give to the truthful Yogin the five tattvas (wine &c.) sanctified according to the kula way'. Then [[&|a]] mantra of ten syllables is declared '[[hrim srim krim parama. svari 170$|hrīm śrīm krīm parameśvari]][^1708] [[syābā'|svāhā']], by merely listening to which a man becomes [[jivanmukta.|jīvanmukta.]] Then by various combinations of the [[mystio|mystic]] syllables with [[Parameśvari|Parameśvarī]] and Kālikā twelve mantras are produced (V. 18). But the mantras do not confer siddhi unless the kulācāra way is followed viz. the five tattvas [[madya'&o.|'madya' &c.]] [[ara|are]] offered (V. 22-23). Then a [[Gayatri Mantra|Gāyatrī mantra]] is set out (V. 62-63) as [[adyayai vidmabe Paramośvaryai dhimabii tan-nah Kali pracodayat il',|'adyāyai vidmahe Parameśvaryai dhīmahi tan-naḥ Kālī pracodayāt॥',]] which is to be muttered thrice daily. The [[Saṅkhya|Sāṅkhya]] tattvas, [[Prakrti,|Prakṛti,]] Mahat, [[Abankara|Ahaṅkāra]] &c. are welded on to the worship of Śakti and the Vedic mantra [[Hainsaḥ śuoiṣad'|'Haṁsaḥ śuciṣad']] ([[Rg.|Ṛg.]] IV. 40. 5) with the Tāntrik [[bija Hrim|bīja Hrīm]] (V. 197). The Tantra gives directions for the sanctification of flesh (V. 203-208, where [[Rg.|Ṛg.]] I. 22. 20 [['tadvisnoḥ paramam padam'|'tadviṣṇoḥ paramam padam']] is employed), of fish (V. 209-210) where the mantra [['Tryambakam' (Rg. VIII,|'Tryambakam' (Ṛg. VII.]] 59. 12) is employed, of mudrā (V. 211-212 where the mantras [['Tad Viṣpo” paramam'|'Tad Viṣṇoḥ paramam']] and 'tad-viprāso' [[Rg. I. 22.20 21,|Ṛg. I. 22. 20-21,]] are employed) to be offered to [[Devi.|Devī.]] The [[Mabanirvāṇatantra|Mahānirvāṇatantra]]

It may be stated here tbat in the two editions of the welfare there is a difference of a few verses here and there.|सत्यत्रेताद्वापरेषु यथा मद्यादिसेवनम् । कलावपि तथा कुर्यात् कुलधर्मानुसारतः ॥ ...कुलमार्गेण तत्त्वानि शोधितानि च योगिने। ये दद्युः सत्यवचसे न हि तान् बाधते कलिः॥ महानिर्वाण० IV. 56 and 60. It may be stated here that in the two editions of the महानिर्वाण there is a difference of a few verses here and there.]]

[^1708]: [[In the man works the letters of the bijas ir mantras are often indicated in a roundabout or mystic way. One example about the grunha 

fiat fi may be set out bere. TÜFTERSTATTET a sorangart (auferater V. 10); here is prots, r is FR, is wroei, ryjaftog is ayer and this gives the bija ain; i and are described in parcamiento (1. 162-64 ) in a similar way, fi and si are respectively the 1978 of Arar (or w att) and of want. Vide r agus (Tantrik Texts Vol. I. 5-22, pp 26–34 for distrus, pp 35-45 for Hiçantave i. e. for sing and letters of the alphabet from a to ). Every bija mantra must have the bindu on it, as in Hrim, Srim, Krim &c.' ari fratterratura (Araru p 50 on Ferrariunture)Character of Mahānirvānatantra|In the तन्त्र works the letters of the bījas in mantras are often indicated in a roundabout or mystic way. One example about the प्राथमिक-बीज ह्रीं may be set out here. प्राणेशस्तैजसारूढो भेरुण्डा व्योमबिन्दुमान् (महानिर्वाण V. 10); here ह is प्राणेश, र is तेजस्, ई is भेरुण्डा, व्योमबिन्दु is अनुस्वार and this gives the bīja ह्रीम्; श्रीं and क्रीं are described in नित्याषोडशिका० (I. 162-64) in a similar way. ह्रीं and श्रीं are respectively the bījas of माया (or भुवनेश्वरी) and of लक्ष्मी. Vide मातृकानिघण्टु (Tāntrik Texts Vol. I. 5-22, pp 26–34 for बीजनिघण्टु, pp 35-45 for मातृकानिघण्टु i. e. for ओम् and letters of the alphabet from अ to क्ष). Every bīja mantra must have the bindu on it, as in Hrīm, Śrīm, Krīm &c. 'बिन्दुयोगं विना बीजस्यायोगात्' (सेतुबन्ध p 50 on नित्याषोडशिकार्णव).]] 

[[P1059]](18th century) being composed after [[sāktism|Śāktism]] had come into great [[ridioule|ridicule]] and obloquy is rather sober,[^1709] It says that women of good birth should simply smell wine and not drink it, while householder [[sūdhalas|sādhakas]] should drink only as much as is contained in five cups, since by drinking too much [[mon|men]] of good family incur the loss of siddhi, and should drink only so long as the wine quaffed does not make their eyes roll or [[doos|does]] not make their mind confused. As to the last tattva (maithuna) the sādhaka was to confine himself to the woman he chooses as his Śakti (VI. 14) and if his wife is alive he is not to touch another woman with a vicious intent, otherwise he would go to hell.[^1710] In keeping with its desire to present respectability along with [[tantrik practicos,|tāntrik practices,]] the Mahānirvāṇa devotes chapter VIII. to the duties of varṇas and āśramas, the duties of the king, the duties of servants in general, provides[^1711] that persons of all varṇas are to marry within the [[varpa|varṇa]] and dine with [[persong|persons]] of the [[game|same]] varṇa, except when engaged in [[Bhairavi|Bhairavī]] cakra and Tattva-cakra (VIII. 150), when men of all varṇas are like the best brāhmaṇas, and no consideration of the castes of the participants arises nor any question about ucchiṣṭa. It prescribes that no one has the adhikāra to engage in the performance of Tattvacakra unless he is [[&|a]] sādhaka endowed with knowledge of brahma. In that cakra the tattvas (wine and the others) should be collected and placed in front of the [[Devi,|Devī,]] the mantra [['Hamgaḥ' (Rg. IV, 40.5)|'Haṁsaḥ' (Ṛg. IV. 40. 5)]] should be recited over all tattvas and the tattvas should be offered to the Highest Self with the [[verge.|verse]] [['Brahmārpanam brahma havir' (Bhagvadgitā|'Brahmārpaṇam brahma havir' (Bhagvadgītā]] IV. 24 = Mahānirvāṇa VIII. 214)

[^1711]: [[संभाले भैरवीचक्रे सवर्णा विजोसमाः। निवृत्ते भैरवीचक्रे सवर्णाः पृथक पथक भामजातिविचारोस्ति नोछिटाविविवेचनम्। चक्रमध्ये गता वीरा मम रूपा नराख्यया।। चक्रादिनिस्ताः सर्वे स्वस्ववर्णाश्रमोदितान् । लोकयात्रामसिच कुर्यः कर्म पृथक प्रया।। halfautore VIII. 179-180, 197. The verse ga rreto..gh occurs also in love faufa VIII.48-49, stan and there are described in arutataforo VIII, 154-176 and VIII, 204-219 respectively.|संप्रवृत्ते भैरवीचक्रे सर्वे वर्णा द्विजोत्तमाः। निवृत्ते भैरवीचक्रे सर्वे वर्णाः पृथक् पृथक् ॥ नामजातिविचारोऽस्ति नोच्छिष्टादिविवेचनम्। चक्रमध्ये गता वीरा मम रूपा नराख्यया॥ चक्रान्निर्गताः सर्वे स्वस्ववर्णाश्रमोदितान् । लोकयात्रामसिद्ध्यर्थं कुर्युः कर्म पृथक् पृथक् ॥ महानिर्वाण० VIII. 179-180, 197. The verse प्रवृत्ते भैरवीचक्रे...पृथक् occurs also in कौलावलीनिर्णय VIII. 48-49. भैरवीचक्र and तत्त्वचक्र are described in महानिर्वाण० VIII. 154-176 and VIII. 204-219 respectively.]]

[[P1060]]and all the [[sadhakas|sādhakas]] should engage in drinking and eating.[^1712] Chap. IX enumerates and describes ten [[sainskāras|saṁskāras]] from [[garbhadhāpa|garbhadhāna]] to marriage for [[membors|members]] of the three varṇas and nine for [[sūdras|śūdras]] (omitting [[upanayanı|upanayana]]), wherein [[Vodic mantrag|Vedic mantras]] are prescribed as in the Dharmasūtras and [[smrtis.|smṛtis.]] [[One interest ing|One interesting]] item is what is called [[Saira|Śaiva]] marriage, which is of two kinds, one entered into according to the rules of cakra and the other lasting for life. It is further provided that in [[Śaiva marriage1713|Śaiva marriage]][^1713] no question of [[rarna|varṇa]] or age arises and that if a man has children from a wife married in the [[brūhma|brāhma]] form and also [[ohildren|children]] of a Śaiva marriage, it is only the former that take as heirs and the latter are entitled only to food and raiment ([[IX 261-264|IX. 261-264]]). Chapters X, XI, XII of the [[Mabānirvāṇa|Mahānirvāṇa]] deal respectively with [[śrūddhas|śrāddhas]], [[prāyaścitlas|prāyaścittas]] for [[sids|sins]] and [[ryjarahāra.|vyavahāra.]]

It is necessary now to turn to the [[Sāradātilaka|Śāradātilaka]] which belongs to about the 11th century A. D. That work is divided into 25 [[patalas|paṭalas]] and contains over 4500 [[verse.|verses.]] It presents in the [[begin. ning|beginning]] a somewhat abstruse and involved philosophy. It says that Śiva is both [[nirguna|nirguṇa]] and [[saquna,|saguṇa,]] the former being different from [[Prakrti,|Prakṛti,]] the latter being [[8ssociated|associated]] with [[Praksti.|Prakṛti.]] Then it describes the order of evolution and manifestation as [[follows:|follows :—]] From the [[Saguna|Saguṇa]] Parameśvara described as [["Sac-cidananda vibhava'|"Sac-cidānanda-vibhava"]] [[Sakti|Śakti]][^1714] proceeds; from the latter arises [[nāda (para )|nāda (para)]] and from nāda arises bindu (para) which is divided into three viz. bindu (apara), [[nāda (apara )|nāda (apara)]] and [[bija;|bīja;]] the first is [[indentified|identified]] with Śiva, [[bija being Sakti and rāda|bīja being Śakti and nāda]] being the coming together of the two (Śiva and [[Sakti).|Śakti).]] [[Sakti|Śakti]] creates the worlds, she is [[sabda-bhahma|śabda-brahma]] ([[I. 56 )|I. 56)]] and is called [[parāsakti|parāśakti]] (I. 52) and [[para deyatā|paradevatā]] (I. 57). She flashes like lightning in the [[adhāra-oakra.|ādhāra-cakra.]][^1715]

[^1712]: [[ततो बाहोण मनुना समर्थ परमात्मने। महासः साधकै सार्ध विदायात्यान. # AETITA foto VIII. 216. He is often used in the sense of Ann; vide फुलार्णव XII. 18, वृरहारीतस्मुति VI. 161, 163. मन्त्र and मनु are both derived from the same root 'man' to thiok. The TET-HE is in the|ततो ब्राह्मेण मनुना समर्प्य परमात्मने । ब्रह्मज्ञैः साधकैः सार्धं विदध्यादात्मभोजनम् ॥ महानिर्वाण० VIII. 216. मनु is often used in the sense of मन्त्र ; vide कुलार्णव XII. 18, वृद्धहारीतस्मृति VI. 161, 163. मन्त्र and मनु are both derived from the same root 'man' to think. The ब्राह्म-मनु is ओं सच्चिदेकं ब्रह्म.]]

II. ed. by Arthur Avalon) verses 4–49, Dakṣiṇā-mūrtisaṁhitā VII. 11–16 for cakras and "Serpent Power" (by A. Avalon, ed, of 1953) which contains an English translation]]

[[P1061]][[Sakti|Śakti]] assumes the form of [[Kuṇdalini|Kuṇḍalinī]] in the human body. From [[Sambhu|Śambhu]] in the form of bindu arise in order [[Sadasiva, Isa. Rudra,|Sadāśiva, Īśa, Rudra,]] Viṣṇu, Brahmā; from the avyakta bindu arise in order the mahat-tattva, [[abankāra|ahaṅkāra]] and the other tattvas mentioned in the [[Sāökbya|Sāṅkhya]] system. [[Sakti|Śakti]] is all-pervading and yet more subtle than the subtlest, she is the [[Kundalini|Kuṇḍalinī]] coiled like a serpent and manifests herself in the form of the fifty letters of the Sanskrit alphabet (from 'a' to 'kṣa').

Before proceeding further some explanation of the six cakras which form an important constituent subject of several tantras is needed. There are said to be six cakras (centres) in the human body viz. [[Adhāra|Ādhāra]] or [[Mūlādhāra ( at|Mūlādhāra (at]] the base of the spine), [[Svadhisthāna ( near|Svādhiṣṭhāna (near]] the generative organ), [[Manipūra ( near|Maṇipūra (near]] the navel), [[Anahata (near the beart),|Anāhata (near the heart),]] [[Visuddha ( near throat),|Viśuddha (near throat),]] and [[Ajõā|Ājñā]] (between the brows). Besides these, there is [[Brahma randhra|Brahmarandhra]] figured as the pericarp of the thousand-petalled lotus

[[P1062]]within the crown of the head. The [[oakres|cakras]] are often [[indentified|identified]] with the nerve plexuses of modern physiology, but the [[descrip tions|descriptions]] in Sanskrit works of the [[Kundalini|Kuṇḍalinī]] and the cakras are meant to refer not to the gross body but rather to what is situated in the subtle body that vanishes when a man dies. The idea of the relevant Sanskrit texts is that the [[Kundalini Sakti ('Kundalin'|Kuṇḍalinī Śakti ('Kuṇḍalin']] [[means & serpent)|means a serpent)]] is asleep in the [[Mūlādhara-oakra|Mūlādhāra-cakra]] coiled like a serpent and has to be roused by the practices of Yoga and deep meditation.[^1716]] The [[Sāradātilaka anks|Śāradātilaka asks]] in an eloquent stanza the [[sādbaka|sādhaka]] to meditate upon the [[Kundalini|Kuṇḍalinī]] which when roused passes from the Mūlādhāra-cakra by means of the [[Susumnā-nadi|Suṣumnā-nāḍī]] (which is in the [[contre|centre]] of the spinal column) through all the six cakras, unites with Śiva in the Sahasrāra (thousand-petalled) cakra and then returns to [[Mlūlādbāra.|Mūlādhāra.]] Each of the six cakras is said to have a certain number of petals [[viz, 4,|viz. 4,]] 6, 10, 12, 16, 2 (50 in all) in order from [[Mūlādbāra|Mūlādhāra]] to [[Ajñā|Ājñā]] (vide Rudrayāmala, 17th [[Patala,|Paṭala,]] verses 55-56). The letters of the alphabet also are 50 [[(from 'a' to * kṣa')|(from 'a' to 'kṣa')]] and they are assigned in groups to the six cakras as follows : ha and kṣa to [[Ajña,|Ājñā,]] 16 vowels to [[Visuddha|Viśuddha]] in the throat, letters ka to [[tha|ṭha]] (12 in all) to [[Anahata,|Anāhata,]] letters [[Da to Pha (10) to Mapipūra,|ḍa to pha (10) to Maṇipūra,]] ba to la (6 in all) to [[Svādbiṣthāna,|Svādhiṣṭhāna,]] letters va to sa (4) to Mūlādhāra. Some [[tantrag|tantras]] state the colour of each of the six cakras and identify them with the five elements and the mind. These speculations of the Yoga and Tantras are

The TEARS (36. 6–168) sets out 1008 names of quafisoft, all of which begin with the lotter .|आधारे लिङ्गनाभिप्रकटितहृदये तालुमूले ललाटे द्वे पत्रे षोडशारे द्विचतुर्दशदलद्वादशार्धं चतुष्के। नासान्ते भालमध्ये डफकठसहिते कण्ठदेशे स्वराणां हं क्षं तत्त्वार्थयुक्तं सकलदलगतं वर्णरूपं नमामि ॥ ; that nectar flows over कुण्डलिनी when it reaches सहस्रार is stated in verse 47 of the same 'प्रकाशामानां प्रथमे प्रयाणे प्रतिप्रयाणेऽप्यमृतायमानाम् । अन्तः पदव्यामनुसञ्चरन्तीमानन्दरूपामबलां प्रपद्ये ॥ ; मूलोन्निद्रभुजङ्गराजमहिषीं यान्तीं सुषुम्नान्तरं भित्त्वाधारसमूहमाशु विलसत्सौदामिनी सन्निभाम् ॥ व्योमाम्भोजगतेन्दुमण्डलगलद्दिव्यामृतौघप्लुतां सम्भाव्य स्वगृहं गतां पुनरिमां सञ्चिन्तयेत्कुण्डलीम् ॥ शारदा० 25 65; vide ibid. 25. 78 for the वर्णs assigned to the six चक्रs. मूल and स्वगृह in (verse 65) mean the मूलाधारचक्र and भुजङ्गराजमहिषी means कुण्डलिनी. Vide षट्चक्रनिरूपण verse 53 also, for the stream of nectar flowing over कुण्डलिनी in सहस्रारपद्म. Vide मन्त्रमहोदधि IV. 19-25, ज्ञानार्णवतन्त्र (24. 45-54), महानिर्वाणतन्त्र V. 113-115, for the number of petals in cakras, their colours, the letters assigned to each and their identification with the five elements and mind and सौन्दर्यलहरी verse 9 'महीं मूलाधारे...सहस्रारे पद्मे सह रहसि पत्या विहरसे ॥' for identifying the five elements and the mind with the six cakras. In 'Sarasvatībhavana Studies' Vol. II, pp. 83-92 Pandit Gopināth Kavirāja describes the system of cakras according to Gorakṣanātha. The रुद्रयामल (36. 6-168) sets out 1008 names of कुण्डलिनी, all of which begin with the letter क.]]

[[P1063]]developments of the ancient Upaniṣad theories briefly noticed [[below,|below.]][^1717]]

Letters form words and words form mantras, that are the power incarnate of [[Sakti.|Śakti.]] Then the [[Sāradātilaka|Śāradātilaka]] describes [[āgana, maṇḍapa, kuṇda, mandala, pithas|āsana, maṇḍapa, kuṇḍa, maṇḍala, pīṭhas]] (on which images of gods are to be placed), [[diksa|dīkṣā]] (initiation), [[prāṇapratiṣthā|prāṇapratiṣṭhā]] (vivifying images), production of sacrificial fire. The [[sāradā tilaka|Śāradātilaka]] (I. 109 and V. 81-91), the Varivasyārahasya ([[II..80),|II. 80),]] the Paraśurāmakalpasūtra (I. 4, [['Ṣat-trimsat tattyāni visvam')|'Ṣaṭ-triṁśat tattvāni viśvam')]] and other Tāntrik and Āgamic works enumerate 36 [[tattyag|tattvas]] (including those of the [[sānkhya|sāṅkhya]] system). From [[chap. VII. to XXIII|chap. VII to XXIII]] mantras of different deities, their formation, use and [[resulta,|results,]] abhiṣekas and mudrās are described. Yantras are dealt with in chap. XXIV and Yoga in chap. XXV. It must be said to the credit of the [[Sāradātilaka|Śāradātilaka]] that it treats of only mantras and mudrās and hardly anywhere treats of the other makāras. The [[Sāradātilaka|Śāradātilaka]] has been profusely quoted as an authoritative Tantra by medieval Dharmaśāstra writers like [[Govindananda,|Govindānanda,]] Raghunandana, [[Kamalakara, Nilakantha, Mitramiśra and orthers.|Kamalākara, Nīlakaṇṭha, Mitramiśra and others.]] In J. G. J. R. I. vol. III. pp. 97-108 M. M. [[Gopinath|Gopīnāth]] Kavirāja contributes a learned paper on nāda, bindu and kalā and takes great pains to elucidate these and hopes that his exposition will make the meaning of these words clear (p. 103 ). But the author feels grave doubts whether most readers will find the meanings clear.

Many tantras speak of the five [[makāres|makāras]] as the means of worshipping [[Devi,|Devī,]] as enabling a man to possess miraculous

III. 108 also says the same.]]

[[P1064]]powers and as leading to final liberation. The [[Kularnava stater * the great|Kulārṇava states 'the great]] Bhairava has [[proscribed|prescribed]] that in the Kaula system [[Biddhi|Siddhi]] (perfection) results from those very substances by (resorting to) which (ordinarily) men incur sin'. That means that the Kaula system eradicates poison by poison or, to employ modern terminology, its principles are like those of Homeopathy.[^1718]

The Tantras seem to be not unaware that in prescribing the five makāras as leading to mukti (final liberation) they were playing with fire. The [[Kulārṇave|Kulārṇava]] itself remarks (II. 117-119 and 122) 'If by merely drinking wine a man were to attain siddhi (miraculous powers, perfection), then all wretched drunkards may attain siddhi. If, by merely eating meat a holy goal were to be secured then all meat-eaters in the world would be holy men. If by mere intercourse with a woman (called [[sakti)|śakti)]] [[moksa|mokṣa]] was to result, then all men in the world may attain liberation. To follow the path (of Kula) is indeed more unattainable than walking on the edge of a sword, than clinging to a tiger's neck, than holding a serpent (in one's hand)'. The Kulārṇava prefaces the preceding dicta by the words 'Many, who are devoid of traditional knowledge and who profane (the [[sāstra|śāstra]]) by false ideas, imagine that the Kaulika [[dootrine|doctrine]] is this and that, relying on their (poor) intellect' (II. 116).

The [[Devibhāgavata|Devībhāgavata]] provides (XI. 1. 25) that so much of tantra as is not opposed to the Veda is undoubtedly [[authorita. tive ( vedāvirodhi cot tantram tat pramāṇam na samsayaḥ),|authoritative (vedāvirodhi cet tantram tat pramāṇaṁ na saṁśayaḥ),]] but what is opposed to Veda is not authoritative.

There appears to have been great rivalry between the Hindu and Buddhist tantras. The [[Saktisangama-tantra,|Śaktisaṅgama-tantra,]] one of the most popular and most exhaustive works on tantra, states that [[Dovi|Devī]] manifests herself for the destruction of Bauddha and other heretical sects, for the removal of the confusing admixture of

[^1718]: [[gadi zut: Pere alrea strateaza amery AETATI orta V. 48; vide Mar (Buddbist tantra) I, 15 Auta NT: कल्पकोदिशतान्यपि। पच्यन्ते नरके घोरे सेन योगी विमुच्यते ॥ ' and compare प्रज्ञोपाय. (Badabist) V. p. 23 verses 24-25 anni prima di tangan Fiter my gy a FITETET:NI (botb works in 'Two vajrayana texts,' G.O.S.). Bagchi in 'Studies in tantras' (pp 36-37) abows that according to some Tantrik works the words जनयित्री, स्वसु, भागिनेयी have esoteric meanings and not the ordinary meaning. But in the context in which they are employed in the two vajrayāda toxts, it is rather difficult to hold that they are employed in any esoteric or symbolic sense.|यैरेव पतनं द्रव्यैः सिद्धिरतैरेव चोदिता । श्रीकौलदर्शने चापि भैरवेण महात्मना ॥ कुलार्णव V. 48; vide ज्ञानसिद्धि (Buddhist tantra) I. 15 'कर्मणा येन वै सत्त्वाः कल्पकोटिशतान्यपि । पच्यन्ते नरके घोरे तेन योगी विमुच्यते ॥ ' and compare प्रज्ञोपाय० (Buddhist) V. p. 23 verses 24-25 'जनयित्रीं स्वसारं च स्वपुत्रीं भागिनेयिकाम् । कामयन् तत्त्वयोगेन लघु सिध्येत साधकः ॥ ' (both works in 'Two vajrayāna texts,' G. O. S.). Bagchi in 'Studies in tantras' (pp 36-37) shows that according to some Tāntrik works the words जनयित्री, स्वसृ, भागिनेयी have esoteric meanings and not the ordinary meanings. But in the context in which they are employed in the two vajrayāna texts, it is rather difficult to hold that they are employed in any esoteric or symbolic sense.]]

[[P1065]][[different culty,|different cults,]] for the establishment of the (true) [[oult,|cult,]] for the protection of what the brāhmaṇas stand for and for the perfect attainment of mantra-śāstra.[^1719] The Bauddha tantras, on the other hand, were not slow to retaliate.

It would not be entirely out of place if [[& fow|a few]] words were devoted to the Bauddha Tantras, particularly of the Vajrayāna. It has already been shown above (pp. 943–44 note: 1516) that all Buddhists, whether of the [[hinayāna'|'hīnayāna']] or mahāyāna type, were required to observe strict rules and regulations such as those of [[pancasilas|pañcaśīlas]] and of taking refuge with Buddha, Dharma and Sangha and of [[daśasllas|daśaśīlas]] (for monks). The goal of [[nirvana|nirvāṇa]] (particularly under Mahāyāna doctrines) could be reached only after a very long time or after several births. As luxuries viz. flesh, fish, wine and women had been banned, the general mass of the people and probably monks also were tired of the strict mode of life and long waiting for the goal. Buddhist Tantras like the Guhyasamāja (that belongs to the Vajrayāna school) provided an easy process by which liberation and even [[Buddha.. bood|Buddha-hood]] could be secured in a short time and even in[^1720] one life and averred that Bodhisattvas and Buddhas attained the seat of dharma by enjoying all objects of pleasure as they[^1721] pleased. The word Vajra means both 'diamond' and 'thunderbolt'. The first meaning appears to have been principally meant in the Guhyasamāja but the 2nd meaning also must have been [[some times|sometimes]] intended. Vajra denotes anything which is hard like a diamond. In the Guhyasamājatantra the word Vajra, either singly or in compounds, [[Occurs|occurs]] hundreds of times. [[Kaya (body), Vak|Kāya (body), Vāk]] (speech) and citta (mind) are called [['trivajra' (Gulya,|'trivajra' (Guhya,]] pp. 31, 35, 36, 43). Numerous other things[^1722] are called 'vajra',

S. pp. 23,37).|शून्यता is called वज्र because it is दृढं सारमसौ (शी?) र्यमच्छेद्याभेद्यलक्षणम् । अदाहि अविनाशि च शून्यता वज्रमुच्यते ॥ अद्वयवज्रसंग्रह (G. O. S. pp. 23, 37).]] This is somewhat like the doctrine of Brahma and [[Atman|Ātman]] in the [[Bhagavadgita|Bhagavadgītā]] II. 23-25 ([[नैनं छिन्दन्ति शमाणि&c.).ज्ञानसिदि.76|नैनं छिन्दन्ति शस्त्राणि &c.). ज्ञानसिद्धि p. 76]] explains: [[सर्वसवेषु महाकरणाममाणानुगतं बोधिचित्तं वनइत्यर्थ:/.e. पन and पोधिचिच (Enlightenment)|सर्वसत्त्वेषु महाकरुणामप्रमाणानुगतं बोधिचित्तं वज्रमित्यर्थः; i. e. वज्र and बोधिचित्त (Enlightenment)]] are synonymous, [[न इयं नाइयं शान्तं शिवं सर्वत्र संस्थितम् । प्रत्यात्मवेयमचल प्रज्ञोपायमनाकुलम् ॥ प्रझोपाय. I. 20: प्रज्ञापारमिता सेन्या सर्वथा सचि काक्षिभिः...ललनारूपमास्थाय सर्ववव ज्यवस्थिता। अतोथै पचनाधेन प्रोतापामासम्मवा। मजोपाय V. 22-23.|न द्वयं नाद्वयं शान्तं शिवं सर्वत्र संस्थितम् । प्रत्यात्मवेद्यमचलं प्रज्ञोपायमनाकुलम् ॥ प्रज्ञोपाय० I. 20; प्रज्ञापारमिता सेव्या सर्वथा मुक्ति-काङ्क्षिभिः ।...ललनारूपमास्थाय सर्वत्रैव व्यवस्थिता। अतोऽर्थे वज्रनाथेन प्रोक्ताप्यन्याङ्गसम्भवा ॥ प्रज्ञोपाय० V. 22-23.]]

[[P1066]]such as the [[sunya|śūnya]] (the Absolute of the [[Madhyamaka|Mādhyamaka]] school) and [[ako Vijñans|also Vijñāna]] (consciousness ), which is the [[sele|sole]] reality according to [[tho|the]] [[Yogāoāra|Yogācāra]] school, and the [[Mabāsukha (Bliss )|Mahāsukha (Bliss)]] added by the [[Śaktas.|Śāktas.]] It also means the male organ in the mystic language of [[Saktas.|Śāktas.]] Though the original Buddhist rules insisted on [[ahimga,|ahiṁsā,]] Guhyasamāja permitted several kinds of flesh, such as that of elephants, horses, dogs and even human[^1723] flesh. Early Buddhism insisted upon truthfulness and continence ([[brahma cerya);|brahmacarya);]] vajrayāna, being an innovating revolt, allowed the killing of all animals, speaking untruth, intercourse with women (including even incest with the mother, sister[^1724] and daughter) and [[appropriating:wealth|appropriating wealth]] not donated by any one. This was called vajramārga (the path of vajra), which is said to have been the [[dootrine|doctrine]] of all Buddhas.

The Prajñopāya (I. 20 ) describes the [[stato|state]] reached by the Vajrayāna method as follows. 'It is neither duality [[por 100, duality,|nor non-duality,]] it is full of peace, beneficent, present everywhere, to be realised by one's own self, steady (unchanging), undisturbed and full of [[Prajina|Prajñā]] (wisdom) and [[Upaya|Upāya]] (activity with [[compas.|compassion)]]

[^1723]: [[. 1723. मांसाहाराविकृत्या महामांसं प्रकल्पयेत् ।...हस्तिमांस हवमासं सानासं ज्योसमम्। भदाहारकुत्यार्थ न चान्यनु विभक्षयेत्। प्रियो भवति खाना बोषितलब धीमताम् । अनेन खल गोगेन लघु शुद्धतमामयात्। एसमाज- 6th पटल P. 26; vide शानसिद्धि of इन्द्रभूति I. 12-14 for similar verses, प्राणिनखया पास्या वक्तपंथ सुपा पचः अवचं च त्वया ग्राह्यं सेवनं योषितामपि.|मांसाहारादिकृत्यार्थं महामांसं प्रकल्पयेत् ।...हस्तिमांसं हयमांसं श्वानमांसं तथोत्तमम् । भक्ष्याहारकृत्यार्थं न चान्यत्तु विभक्षयेत् । प्रियो भवति बुद्धानां बोधिसत्त्वश्च धीमताम् । अनेन खलु योगेन लघु बुद्धत्वमाप्नुयात् । गुह्यसमाज० 6th पटल p. 26; vide ज्ञानसिद्धि of इन्द्रभूति I. 12-14 for similar verses, प्राणिनश्च त्वया घात्या वक्तव्यं च मृषा वचः । अदत्तं च त्वया ग्राह्यं सेवनं योषितामपि ॥]]

[^1724]: [[1924. अनेन पत्रमार्गेण वज्रसस्वान् प्रचोदयेत् । एपो हि सर्वहदानां समयः परम शाच रायसा, 16th पटल P 120; ये परतण्याभिरता नित्य कामरताच ये...माभगिनी पुचीच कामयेद्यस्त साधकः। स सिद्धि विपुलां गच्छेत् महायानामधर्मता। एस. 5th पडल p.30: 'सर्वाइकुत्सितायां वा न कुर्यादवमाननाम्। भियं सर्वकुलोत्पमा पूजयेद पत्रधारिणीर चण्डालकुलसम्मूतो डोम्भिको वा विशेषतः। शुसुप्सितकुलोत्पमा सेवयद सिद्धिमापाद। ज्ञानसिदि. 80 and 82. Das-Gupta in 'Introduction to tantrit Buddhtam' P. 114.|अनेन वज्रमार्गेण वज्रसत्त्वान् प्रचोदयेत् । एषो हि सर्वबुद्धानां समयः परम-शाश्वतः ॥ गुह्यस०, 16th पटल p 120; ये परद्रव्याभिरता नित्यं कामरताश्च ये ।...मातृभगिनी-पुत्रीश्च कामयेद्यस्तु साधकः । स सिद्धिं विपुलां गच्छेन् महायानाग्रधर्मताम् ॥ गुह्यस. 5th पटल p. 20; 'सर्वाङ्गकुत्सितायां वा न कुर्यादवमाननाम् । स्त्रियं सर्वकुलोत्पन्नां पूजयेद् वज्रधारिणीम् ॥ चण्डालकुलसम्भूतां डोम्बिकां वा विशेषतः । जुगुप्सितकुलोत्पन्नां सेवयन् सिद्धिमाप्नुयात् ।' ज्ञानसिद्धि pp. 80 and 82. Vide Dr. Guenther's exposition of this passage and of a similar one from Prajñopāya. V. 25 in 'Yuganaddha' pp. 106-109., Vide Dr. S. B. Das-Gupta in 'Introduction to tāntrik Buddhism' p. 114.]]

[[P1067]]sion) and it further provides (V. 22-23) that 'by those who hanker after liberation perfection of wisdom must be sought (resorted to or adored) in all ways. This perfection of wisdom is everywhere present assuming the form of woman'. [[Prajña|Prajñā]] was linked up with an intense state of emotion called Sukha or Mahāsukha (great bliss). "It being of the nature of endless bliss is designated mahāsukha; it is beneficent all round, most eminent and conduces to complete enlightenment' (Prajñopāya. I. 27) and 'this all Buddha knowledge which is by its nature to be experienced by one's inmost self is called Mahāsukha (great bliss) since it is the most eminent of all pleasures' (Jñānasiddhi VII. 3). The word Prajñā is feminine in [[gendør|gender]] and therefore some Vajrayāna writers identified Prajñā with woman; by [[erotio|erotic]] symbolism and far-fetched analogies the cult of woman was started.

Dr. H. V. Guenther has published a book styled 'Yuganaddha' propounding the Tāntrik view of life based only on Buddhist Tantras and endeavours to prove in that work (of about 190 pages) that the Buddhist Tāntrikas try to restore life in its entirety, which is neither an indulgence in passions nor a rejection and escape, but a complete reconciliation to the hard facts of life, that the sexual aspect of the Tantras is but the corrective against the one-sided intellectualism and rationalism of mere philosophy which is unable to cope with the problems of everyday life and that the symbol of Yuganaddha points to the unique harmony and interpenetration of masculinity and [[feminity,|femininity,]] of blunt truth and symbolic truth, of intellect and humanity. It is not possible even to summarize this work or criticize it here. The core of the doctrine of the Vajrayāna tantras is found in the passages quoted in notes 1720-21, [[1723-84.|1723-24.]] The argument is: according to these Tantras perception of wholeness is the most joyous of all human experiences and man's experience will not be full but only partial if he has [[dio|no]] experience of [[feminity|femininity]] i. e. of everything [[fomale.|female.]] He may [[expertendo feminity|experience femininity]] through all female members of his family. [[Thorefore,|Therefore,]] it is not to be wondered at, says Dr. Guenther, that 'this [[er. perionoe|ex-perience]] so often has an incestuous character'. Then he offers (on pp. [[106-11%)|106-112)]] a lengthy explanation of what he means which, the [[prosont|present]] author has to [[confoss,|confess,]] is not quite clear to his [[moderato|moderate]] mental abilities. Dr. Guenther appears to be steeped in all the latest theories of modern psychologists like those of Freud and tries to expound that Buddhist [[author|authors]] of the 8th century A. D. [[Like Anangavafra and Indrabhu had plumbed the depths at the|like Anaṅgavajra and Indrabhūti had plumbed the depths of the]]

[[P1068]]psychical life on the lines of modern psycho-analysis. Granting for a moment all that Dr. Guenther says about bi-sexuality, about sexual partnership being the best expression for the most intimate relation between two opposites, about woman being for the [[malo|male]] a material object and a goddess, the present writer feels that one question appears to have been not satisfactorily answered or explained, viz. why did not the Buddhist Tāntrikas simply exhort the sādhaka to understand the emotions, [[view points|view-points]] and value of woman as a mother, sister, wife, daughter or as a woman in general and why did they frequently and blatantly harp upon sexual intercourse of even an incestuous kind as a quick method of realizing the goal ?

The [[Guhyagamāja-tantra|Guhyasamāja-tantra]] puts forward a quick and short method for realising Buddhahood and for the attainment of miraculous powers (siddhis) through the processes of Yoga. The siddhis are said to be of two kinds, Sāmānya (ordinary,[^1725] such as becoming invisible) and Uttama (highest i. e. attaining Buddhahood). Four means for securing ordinary siddhis are mentioned and they are called [[Vajra-catuska.|Vajra-catuṣka.]] It is further provided that the best [[ṣiddhi|siddhi]] is acquired by the nectar of knowledge due to the six [[angas|aṅgas]][^1726] of Yoga (and by no other way) viz. [[Pratyābāra,|Pratyāhāra,]] Dhyāna, Prāṇāyāma, Dhāraṇā, Anusmṛti and [[Samādbi.|Samādhi.]] It is worthy of note that the first three [[angas|aṅgas]] of yoga mentioned in the [[Yogagātra|Yogasūtra]] viz. yama, niyama[^1727] and [[asana|āsana]] are omitted and a new one, Anusmṛti, is added. Yama could not be included because to the Guhyasamāja it did not matter if the sādhaka ate flesh, or indulged in sexual intercourse or told lies, while in Yogasūtra yamas are ahiṁsā, [[salya|satya]] (truthfulness), asteya (not appropriating anything that does not belong to one), brahmacarya (sexual purity) and aparigraha (non-acceptance of gifts). [[Niyames|Niyamas]] could not be [[inoluded|included]] because among the five

[[P1069]]niyamas are svādhyāya (Veda study) and ([[Isvaraprapidhāna )|Īśvarapraṇidhāna)]] devotion to or surrender to God. Many Bauddhas reviled the Veda and acknowledged no Supreme Deity. The Guhyasamāja introduced Yogic practices for quickly securing Buddhahood. The idea of allowing flesh and sexual intercourse seems to have been that the Yogin is to be indifferent as to what he does so long as he is striving for the goal of Buddhahood, for development of his psychic life and that he may disregard all social [[conyon tions|conventions]] and rules.[^1728] Another innovation of the Vajrayāna was the introduction of upāsanā of [[Sakti|Śakti]] for liberation through Yoga. The Guhya-samāja provides that if even after making efforts for six months, the sādhaka has no realisation, [[ho|he]] may repeat his [[offorts|efforts]] for three times more and if even after that he has no enlightenment he should resort to [[Hathayoga|Haṭhayoga]] and then he would attain correct knowledge through Yoga. A further innovation was the theory of the five Dhyāni-buddhas,[^1729] emanating from the Bhagavān, who represent the five Skandhas or fundamental principles of which the whole creation is composed and each of whom was associated with a female [[Sakti.|Śakti.]] The teaching of the Guhyasamāja is that if psychical power and miraculous siddhis are to be developed females must be associated with those who undertake Yogic exercises to achieve their [[enda.|ends.]] Thus the prophecy of the founder of Buddhism pronounced, when yielding to the pressure and entreaties of his favourite disciple [[Ananda|Ānanda]] for allowing women to be members of the sangha and to be nuns, that on account of this innovation his system would [[atand|stand]] fast only for five hundred years, though otherwise it would have lasted for a thousand years, was literally fulfilled (vide Cullavagga

[^1729]: [[Vido|Vide]] Dr. Bhattacharya's Introduction to [[Gabyasamājartantta|Guhyasamāja-tantra]] p. XIX and [[bis|his]] Intro. to B. E. [[Pp.|pp.]] 32-33, 70, 80–81, 121, 128-130 for the theory of [[Dhyaal-buddhas,|Dhyāni-buddhas,]] their [[faktis,|śaktis,]] kulas, the meaning of [[kola|kula]] &c. On [[P.|p.]] 32 of B. E. Dr. Bhattacharya observes [[' we|'we]] have already mentioned that [[Buddbism|Buddhism]] was a challenge to and repudiation of earlier Brahmanism. It was now the part of [[Tāatrik|Tāntrik]] Buddhism to challenge the authority of Buddha and [[repadiate|repudiate]] the original [[Buddhlam.|Buddhism.]] All [[klads|kinds]] of worldly [[enjoymento|enjoyments]] were forbidden (by Buddha), especially wine, [[Womon,|women,]] fish, meat [[rod.|and]] exciting food. All [[those|these]] the [[Tāntrikas of the later age fatrodaood into their raligion and avon went so far as to declare that without thoaa emancipadon was impossible',|Tāntrikas of the later age introduced into their religion and even went so far as to declare that without these emancipation was impossible'.]]

[[P1070]]X. 1. 6 in Vinaya Texts, vol. III, S. B. E. XX. p. 325). If we accept 483 [[B. O.|B. C.]] as the date of Buddha's [[Parinirvana ( as|Parinirvāṇa (as]] many scholars hold) or 477 B. C. (as A. Foucher [[bolds)|holds)]] 500 years therefrom would bring us to the first century A. D. and it is clear that by a century or two after that time much of Buddha's teaching appears to have been almost completely swamped by doctrines of Mahāyāna and Vajrayāna Tantras. By a strange irony of fate Buddha's 'dharma-cakra-pravartana' came to be transformed into 'adharma-cakra-pravartana' by many of his so-called followers of the [[Vajrayana.|Vajrayāna.]] In the [[Mabā-parinibbana autta|Mahā-parinibbāna-sutta]] V. 23 (S. B. E. vol. XI. [[p. 91 )|p. 91)]] the Buddha was very strict and warned bhikkhus not to see [[bhikkhunis,|bhikkhunīs,]] not to talk to them if they could not avoid seeing them, and to be wide awake if [[&|a]] nun talked to them. Buddha severely rebuked one of his disciples for showing his miraculous powers (vide p. 1037 and [[2. 1672|n. 1672]] above), but [[Guhyagamāja|Guhyasamāja]] and other Buddhist tantras make provision for endowing the [[sādbaka|sādhaka]] with [[miraculoug|miraculous]] powers, such as causing rainfall in case of drought ([[Guhya Bamāja|Guhyasamāja]] p. 84), killing an enemy by magical rites over an effigy of the enemy (ibid. p. 96). Besides, the Guhyasamāja knows the [[gix|six]] cruel or terrible magic acts (called [['satkarmāṇi),|'ṣaṭkarmāṇi),]] viz. [[Sānti|Śānti]] (rite for averting disease or black magic), [[vaśikarana|vaśīkaraṇa]] (bewitching women and men and even [[gods ),|gods),]] stambhana (stopping the movements or actions of others), [[vidveṣana|vidveṣaṇa]] (creating enmity between two friends or two persons who love each [[other ),|other),]] [[uccātana|uccāṭana]] (making a person or enemy flee from the country, town or village), [[mārana|māraṇa]] (killing or causing permanent injury to living beings). The [[Guhyasamaja|Guhyasamāja]] mentions these six ([[putting * Akarṣaṇa ' for 'vidveṣana')|putting 'ākarṣaṇa' for 'vidveṣaṇa')]] respectively at pp. 168, 165, 96, 87 ([[Akarṣaña),|Ākarṣaṇa),]] 81, 130. Vide [[Sadhanamālā|Sādhanamālā]] pp. 368-369 for the same and for the shape of [[mandalas|maṇḍalas]] and times for each of the six cruel rites. Even the rather [[sobor Saradātilaka-tantra mentiong|sober Śāradātilaka-tantra mentions]] these six (23. [[12%),|122),]] defines them (23. 123-125), provides that Rati, [[Vāṇi ( speech or Sarasvati),|Vāṇī (speech or Sarasvatī),]] Ramā, [[Jyoṣths,|Jyeṣṭhā,]] Durgā and [[Kāli|Kālī]] are respectively the six deities of these six cruel acts and must be worshipped at the commencement, that six periods of ten ghaṭikās from [[suprise|sunrise]] are respectively appropriate to these six and so also are certain seasons (23. 126-139). It is most astounding that the [[Prapaõoasāra|Prapañcasāra]] (23. 5) [[asoribed|ascribed]] to the great advaita teacher [[Saṅkardoārya|Śaṅkarācārya]] describes at length a mantra called Trailokya-mohana for accomplishing the [[aboy si|above six]] cruel acts.


[[P1071]]Both Hindu and Buddhist [[Tantrag1730|Tantras]][^1730] lay great emphasis on the importance and qualifications of the guru. The Bauddha Tantras have the highest praise for the guru. The [[Jūlnasiddhi (13.9-12) proscribes|Jñānasiddhi (13. 9–12) prescribes]] high qualifications and the [[Prajiopāyavini soaya-siddhi (III, 9-16 )|Prajñopāyaviniścaya-siddhi (III. 9–16)]] contains a grand eulogy of the guru, identifying him with Buddha and calling him omniscient and [[80|so]] on. The Advayasiddhi of [[Laksmiðkarā|Lakṣmīṅkarā]] (about 729 A. D.), who started the astounding doctrine that one should offer worship to one's own body wherein all gods reside, says that in the three worlds there is none higher than the ācārya. In the [[Nityotgava|Nityotsava]] of Umānanda-nātha, pupil of [[Bhāgurānanda-nātha (i. . Bhāskararāya|Bhāsurānanda-nātha (i. e. Bhāskararāya]] as he was called before he took [[diksa)|dīkṣā)]] the guru Bhāskararāya is praised in the following hyperbolical [[terms 1731|terms]][^1731] 'to whom no part of the earth was unseen (owing to his yogic sight), there was no king who was not his slave, to whom no śāstra was unknown; why use more words, whose form was the highest [[Sakti|Śakti]] herself.' But the [[Jõāna-siddhi|Jñāna-siddhi]] and [[Kulārpava (XII. 128 ) ward|Kulārṇava (XIII. 128) warn]] against gurus who falsely pretend to know the truth and give instruction in dharma from greed for money. The [[Kulārpava ( ullāsas XI and XII)|Kulārṇava (ullāsas XII and XIII)]] are devoted to the [[quali fications|qualifications]] and greatness of guru. The [[Sāradātilaka|Śāradātilaka]] also sets out the qualifications of the tāntrika guru (II. [[142–144 )|142–144)]] and of the disciple [[(:III. 145–152).|(III. 145–152).]][^1732] The guru among other matters 'must know the essence of all Āgamas and the principles and meaning of all śāstras, he must be one whose words come out true, who has a quiet mind, who has profoundly studied the Veda and its meaning, who follows the path of Yoga and whose bearing is as beneficent as that of a deity.' Among the many requirements about a disciple,[^1733] one is that he must keep secret the mantra and [[pājā|pūjā]] imparted by his guru. The disciple places the guru's feet on his head and surrenders his body, wealth and even life to

[[P1072]]the guru. The necessity of a guru for the [[aoquisition|acquisition]] of [[esotorio|esoteric]] philosophy is [[stronged|stressed]] by the Upaniṣads also. [[For, example|For example,]] the [[Kathopaniṣad|Kaṭhopaniṣad]][^1734] says 'this knowledge cannot be obtained by ratiocination, it can be understood well only when expounded by another'; the [[Chandogy& (IV.9.3)|Chāndogya (IV. 9. 3)]] says 'Sir, I have heard from men like you that only knowledge that is learnt from a teacher leads to real good'. The [[Lingapurana1735|Liṅgapurāṇa]][^1735] and others say that guru is identical with Śiva and the rewards of devotion to Śiva and to the guru are the same. The [[Kularnava (XI.46)|Kulārṇava (XI. 46)]] emphasizes that the order of the succession of gurus, the [[Āgamus,|Āgamas,]] Āmnāya, mantra and [[practiceg-all|practices—all]] these when learnt from the lips of a guru become fruitful and not otherwise. The [[Prapanca. sāra|Prapañcasāra]] provides 'the disciple should consider in his mind that guru, devatā and mantra are one and should repeat a hundred times the mantra that he received by the favour of the guru.'

The Vedānta system requires for its understanding high intellectual and moral attainments and could be followed by only a few gifted souls. It is claimed that Tantras provide [[&|a]] method which assists [[mon|men]] of ordinary intellect and which utilizes visual and physical processes for the attainment of spiritual experience, for the development of [[psychio|psychic]] forces and for the quick attainment of liberation by [[suoh|such]] practices as the repetition of mantras, mudrās, [[nyasa,|nyāsa,]] [[mandalas,|maṇḍalas,]] cakras and yantras. The respect for the guru sometimes reached [[amongat Tāntrik writors|amongst Tāntrik writers]] to extreme and disgusting lengths as the note below will [[indicate.1736|indicate.]][^1736]

[^1735]: [[यो गुहास शिवः प्रोक्तो यः शिवास गुरुः स्मृतः। यथा शिवस्तया विद्या पण विधा तथा एकः॥ शिव-विद्यागुरोस्तस्माद् भक्त्या च सदृशं फलम् । सर्वदेवनयो देवि सर्वशक्तिमयो हि सः॥ लिङ्गपुराण I. 85. 164-165; गुरुर्ब्रह्मा गुरुविष्णुर्गुरुर्देवो महेश्वरः। गुरुरेव परं ब्रह्म तस्मै श्रीगुरवे नमः॥ देवीभागवत XI, 1, 49; in the ललितापाख्यान in the ब्रह्माण्डपु. it is said मनुष्यचर्मणा बद्धः साक्षात्परशिवः स्वयम् । सच्छिष्यान् ग्रहमोक्षार्थ गूढं पर्यटति क्षितौ ॥ अत्रिनेत्रः शिवः साक्षाच्चतुर्बाहुरच्युतः । अचतुर्वदनो ब्रह्मा श्रीगुरुः कथितः प्रिये ॥ 43. 68-70. These verses from ललितोपाख्यान are the same as कुलार्णव XIII.54 and 57 and several verses are common to both. The question as to who is the borrower is very difficult to decide, The शारदातिलक (V. 113-114 ) has ' गुरुविद्यादेवतानामैक्य सम्भावयन् धिया । प्रणमेद् दण्डवद्भूमौ हथ तं देवतात्मकम् । तस्य पादाम्बुजद्वन्द्वं निजे मूर्धनि योजयेत् । शरीरमर्थ प्राणं च सर्व तस्मै निवेदयेत् । गुरुणा समनुगृहीतं मन्त्रं सद्यो जपेच्छतावृत्त्या । गुरुदेवतामनूनामैक्य सम्भावयन् धिया शिष्यः ॥ प्रपञ्चसार VI, 122.|यो गुरुः स शिवः प्रोक्तो यः शिवः स गुरुः स्मृतः। यथा शिवस्तथा विद्या यथा विद्या तथा गुरुः ॥ शिव-विद्यागुरोस्तस्माद् भक्त्या च सदृशं फलम् । सर्वदेवनयो देवि सर्वशक्तिमयो हि सः ॥ लिङ्गपुराण I. 85. 164–165; गुरुर्ब्रह्मा गुरुर्विष्णुर्गुरुर्देवो महेश्वरः । गुरुरेव परं ब्रह्म तस्मै श्रीगुरवे नमः ॥ देवीभागवत XI. 1. 49; in the ललितापाख्यान in the ब्रह्माण्डपु. it is said 'मनुष्यचर्मणा बद्धः साक्षात्परशिवः स्वयम् । सच्छिष्यान्‌ग्रहमोक्षार्थं गूढं पर्यटति क्षितौ ॥ अत्रिनेत्रः शिवः साक्षाच्चतुर्बाहुरच्युतः । अचतुर्वदनो ब्रह्मा श्रीगुरुः कथितः प्रिये ॥' 43. 68–70. These verses from ललितोपाख्यान are the same as कुलार्णव XIII. 54 and 57 and several verses are common to both. The question as to who is the borrower is very difficult to decide. The शारदातिलक (V. 113–114) has 'गुरुविद्यादेवतानामैक्यं सम्भावयन् धिया। प्रणमेद् दण्डवद्‌भूमौ ह्यथ तं देवतात्मकम् ॥ तस्य पादाम्बुजद्वन्द्वं निजे मूर्धनि योजयेत् । शरीरमर्थं प्राणं च सर्वं तस्मै निवेदयेत् ॥' गुरुणा समनुगृहीतं मन्त्रं सद्यो जपेच्छतावृत्त्या। गुरुदेवतामनूनामैक्यं सम्भावयन् धिया शिष्यः ॥ प्रपञ्चसार VI. 122.]]

[^1736]: [[भगिनी वा सुता भायी यो दद्यात्कुलयोगिने । मधुमत्ताय देवेशि तस्य पुण्य न गण्यते ॥ q. byारामनिवार्ण IV. p. 116.|भगिनीं वा सुतां भार्या यो दद्यात्कुलयोगिने । मधुमत्ताय देवेशि तस्य पुण्यं न गण्यते ॥ q. by तारामक्तिसुधार्णव IV. p. 116.]]

[[P1073]]The teaching of Tāntrika texts about the five makāras must have created [[&|a]] very unhealthy and debased state among all classes, particularly the lower orders of Society. The centuries from the 7th to the 12th A. D. were the peak period of Tāntrik works and cults, both Hindu and Buddhist. In [[&|a]] certain cult of the Vajrayāna the gurus wore a blue [[yesture.|vesture.]] A story is narrated of a guru (a monk) of the [[sammitiya|sammitīya]] sect who went dressed in a blue dress to a courtezan. He did not return to the monastery at night. When asked in the morning by his disciples why he put on a blue dress, he explained the great spiritual merit of the blue-coloured dress. Since then his followers began to wear blue dress and in their book called [['Nilapatadarsana'|'Nīlapaṭadarśana']] it is written 'the god Kāmadeva (Eros) is a jewel, a courtezan is [[&|a]] jewel, wine is a [[jowel;|jewel;]] I bow to these three jewels; other so-called jewels are three glass beads'. It should be remembered that for devout Buddhists, Buddha, Dharma and Sangha are three Ratnas (jewels). The followers of the [[Nilapatadarśana|Nīlapaṭadarśana]] deemed these last three to be as worthless as glass beads. Vide Bhikṣu [[Rahula Sāṅkstyāyana's|Rāhula Sāṅkṛtyāyana's]] paper 'On Vajrayāna or [[Mantrayana '|Mantrayāna']] in J. A. vol. 225 (1934) p. 216 where this is narrated. [[Valge|False]] gurus must have gone about deluding people with rosy prospects of bliss and liberation by the easy method of drinking [[wino,|wine,]] eating flesh and free association with all sorts of women. The Indian literature of those centuries is full of condemnation and caricatures of the methods of Tāntrik worship by means of wine, flesh and sexual laxity. A few instances may be cited here. In the [[prākrit|Prākrit]] play called [[Karpūramañjarl of Rajasekhara|Karpūramañjarī of Rājaśekhara]] (about 900 A. D.), a character called [[Bhairavananda 1737|Bhairavānanda]][^1737] who was

[[P1074]][[zupposed|supposed]] to possess wonderful powers is introduced and he [[saya|says]] (while representing that he was somewhat tipsy) 'owing to the favour of our guru we know nothing about mantras or tantras or meditation. We drink wine, dally with women and yet, being devoted to the Kula path, we reach [[mok:a.|mokṣa.]] A fierce strumpet is given dīkṣā and made a lawful wife, wine is drunk and flesh is consumed, our food is got by begging for alms, our bed is a piece of hide. To whom would the Kauladharma not appear attractive ? Even gods headed by Viṣṇu and [[Brahma|Brahmā]] declare that liberation is attained by meditation, Veda study and performance of Vedic sacrifices; only one God, the husband of Umā, perceived that mokṣa [[(oan be attained)|(can be attained)]] by dalliance with women and by wine'. The Yaśastilaka-campū (composed in 959 A. D.) after referring to the Dakṣiṇa and Vāma paths of [[Saivāgama|Śaivāgama]] quotes a [[verge|verse]] of the great poet [[Bhasa|Bhāsa]][^1738] "a person should drink wine, look at the face of the woman dear to him, wear [[&|a]] dress naturally charming and free from being odd; may the adorable Śiva be long-lived, who (first) discovered such a path to mokṣa!" The [[Daśavatāra-carita of Kṣomendra|Daśāvatāra-carita of Kṣemendra]] (3rd quarter of 11th century A. D.) states in one verse what the Tāntrik gurus and their followers did “Gurus declare that liberation follows by the drinking of wine from the same goblet by various craftsmen such as washermen, weavers, workers in hides, [[kāpālais,|kāpālikas,]] in the procedure of cakrapūjā, and by dalliance with women without the least scruple and by always leading a life of festivities."[^1739] The [[Rāja-tarangini|Rāja-taraṅgiṇī]] (middle of 12th

The [[TT|पारानन्दसूत्र]] mentions [[sovoral Tāatrik|several Tāntrik]] teachers whose names [[and in such as area|end in आनन्द such as अच्युतानन्द]] (pp. 54, 73), [[Sparepart|उन्मादानन्द]] (pp. 54, 72, 76), [[marrone|ज्ञानानन्द]] (pp. 54, 73, 91), [[pp|देवानन्द]] (p. 44), [[PETIT|परानन्द]] (pp. 72, 91 the author of [[a), gwire|पारानन्दसूत्र), मुक्तानन्द]] (p. 54), [[u n|सुरानन्द]] (pp. 54, 70, 72). Besides, several teachers have [[tha|the]] word [[as part of their games|भैरव as part of their names]] and are profusely quoted in the [[TURU,|पारानन्दसूत्र,]] viz. [[MTETETTE|आकाशभैरव]] (9 times), [[Far|उन्मत्तभैरव]] (17 times), [[H|कालभैरव]] (11 times); [[has|भैरव as]] an author is [[men tioned|mentioned]] once on p. 66. [[urias|राजशेखर]] probably [[maent a alt|meant a hit]] at one or more of these tāntrik authors who favoured the [[makāras, Acc.|makāras. Acc.]] to [[latro.|Intro.]] (p. XII) to [[ACTIEF,|पारानन्दसूत्र,]] the work was composed probably between 900 to 1200 A. D. The [[परशुराम कल्पसूत्र (I. 40]|परशुराम-कल्पसूत्र (I. 40)]] provides that after दीक्षा the guru is to [[given dame|give a name]] to the disciple ending in [[ITTIC.|आनन्दनाथ.]] The [[Herfaafar|महानिर्वाण]] (X. 182) also says the same thing.

[[P1075]]century A. D.) has several references to the [[Tantrikas|Tāntrikas]] and their doings. In V. 66 [[Kalhana|Kalhaṇa]] says that during[^1740] the reign of Avantivarman of Kashmir, siddhas (persons possessed of [[mira culous|miraculous]] powers like [[animā)|aṇimā)]] such as [[Bhatta Kallata|Bhaṭṭa Kallaṭa]] were born for the benefit of the world. In describing the effects of the rule of a good king [[Yasaskara (1939–948|Yaśaskara (939–948]] A. D.), [[Kalhana 1741|Kalhaṇa]][^1741] remarks that during his reign were not seen housewives figuring as divinities at the rite of [[gurudikṣā|gurudīkṣā]] and shaking their heads for detracting from the eminent character (attributed to) of their husbands.' King [[Kalasa|Kalaśa]] of Kashmir [[(1063-1089|(1063–1089]] A. D.) became a pupil of [[Pramadakantha,|Pramadakaṇṭha,]] son of [[Amarakantha|Amarakaṇṭha]] who was a good [[brahmana,|brāhmaṇa,]] but Kalaśa, who by nature was evil-minded, was instructed in evil practices by his guru [[Pramadakantha|Pramadakaṇṭha]] and the latter made the former ignore the distinction between women who are approachable and who are not. In this connection, [[Kalhana|Kalhaṇa]] bitterly[^1742] bewails "what other unscrupulousness of this guru (of [[Kalasa)|Kalaśa)]] should be described by me, when, leaving aside all scruples, he committed incestuous intercourse with his own daughter." This establishes that in the 11th century A. D.

The guru and bis predecessors are to be worshipped by disciples 28 sacrificers. When the latter praised the busbands of the women that had become guras, they shook their heads in disagreement and thereby impliedly criticized the character of their husbands. Kalbaṇa says that this did not happen in the reiga of Yasaskara, who must have frowned apon the practices of 

tāntricism and bence occasions for women being gurus did not arise.|नादृश्यन्त च गेहिन्यो गुरुदीक्षोत्थदेवताः । कुर्वाणा भर्तृशीलश्रीनिषेधं मूर्धधूननैः ॥ राजत. VI. 12. This shows that, there being equality of sexes among Tāntrikas, women used to be made gurus in Tāntrik rites. Vide प्राणतोषिणी p. 179 for qualifications of a woman guru and p. 540 for worship of the wife of a guru and of a woman as a guru in her own right. The guru and his predecessors are to be worshipped by disciples as sacrificers. When the latter praised the husbands of the women that had become gurus, they shook their heads in disagreement and thereby impliedly criticized the character of their husbands. Kalhaṇa says that this did not happen in the reign of Yaśaskara, who must have frowned upon the practices of tāntricism and hence occasions for women being gurus did not arise.]]

[[P1076]]in Kashmir there were [[Tantrik|Tāntrik]] gurus that literally carried out what the Guhyasamājatantra quoted in note 1724 apparently recommended to Buddhist Yogis. In the drama called [[Moba parajaya|Mohaparājaya]] by [[Yaśabpāla|Yaśaḥpāla]] under king Ajayadeva, successor of [[Kumarapāla,|Kumārapāla,]] among the dramatis personae is a kaula who declares his doctrine to be eating flesh every day, drinking wine without any qualms and allowing the mind free [[scope.1743|scope.]][^1743] Aparārka quotes a verse which shows that, in the midst of numerous cults, it was difficult to be consistent: "A person may be at heart a Kaula (follower of the Kula doctrine), in outward appearance he may look a Śaiva, and he may follow Vaidika rites in his usual practices. One should live grasping what is essential like the [[cocoanut 1741 fruit".|cocoanut fruit]][^1744]". It appears that great scholars and poets had a sneaking admiration for Tāntrika worship. The great writer Vidyāpati of Mithilā appears to be a Vaiṣṇava from his devotional songs, but wrote the [[Saivasarvasvasāra|Śaivasarvasvasāra]] (so he may be called a [[Śaiva ),|Śaiva),]] and wrote also [[Durgābhaktitarangini|Durgābhaktitaraṅgiṇī]] (and thus appears to have been a [[Sākta)|Śākta)]] and composed also a [[Tāntrik 1745 work.|Tāntrik work.]][^1745] The very first verse of Vidyāpati's [['Puruşa parīkṣā'|'Puruṣaparīkṣā']] contains an invocation of [[Adi-Sakti.|'Ādi-Śakti'.]] Bengal and Assam were the strongholds of [[Sākta|Śākta]] doctrines and even now [[Kāli|Kālī]] worship is still in vogue there, but it was the great Bengal
